# امم
meanings are related to direction, leadership, community, and education. It is associated with movement towards something, being a source or origin, leading or following, and belonging to a collective group.  The word أَمَّا and its derivatives are commonly used  for conditional, contrastive, and explanatory purposes.  The verb ammina (أَمَّنَ) means to propose, direct one's steps toward a place, or go towards. Similarly, amama (أَمَامَ) and amam (أَمَام) both mean "before" or "in front of", often used in spatial or positional contexts.  In terms of leadership and authority, imam (إِمَام) (plural: a'immah) refers to a leader, president, or guide, and can also mean any object that is followed, such as a book, road, or example. The verb ta’ammama (تَأَمَّمَ) means to assume leadership or follow an example.  The root also relates to kinship and community. Umm (أُمّ) (plural: ummahat) means mother, source, principle, prototype, or origin. Ummah (أُمَّة) (plural: umam) denotes a community, nation, or people, while ummatun (أُمَّة) refers more specifically to a man's kinsfolk, tribe, party, generation, or the creatures of God.  Regarding knowledge and education, ummiy (أُمِّيّ) describes someone unlettered, belonging to a mother, or an Arab without a revealed scripture of their own. Similarly, ummi (أُمِّيّ) means unlettered or someone without formal scriptural education.  The word "أَمَّا" (Amma) is used in Arabic as a contrastive or conditional word. It introduces a topic and is often followed by "فَـ" (fa) to indicate a result or consequence. For example, "أَمَّا خالدٌ فشجاعٌ" (As for Khalid, he is brave). It can also clarify a subject before giving details, as in "أَمَّا السفرُ فكانَ متعبًا" (As for the trip, it was exhausting).  Another related word, "إمَّا" (Imma), is used to indicate alternatives, similar to "either… or" in English. It often appears in pairs, as in "إمَّا أن تدرسَ، وإمَّا أن تفشلَ" (Either you study, or you will fail).  In summary, the root أ م م encompasses key concepts such as direction, leadership, community, education, and contrastive expressions, making it fundamental in both linguistic and cultural contexts.

# تور
For dara, with the change of ta with dal: To go round, flow, ran, repeat (an action). According to Azhari the word is actually Ta'ara, where Hamza is omitted meaning space of time,a time, one time. Is so called because in its pristine purity, reading it and acting upon its teachings kindled in the heart the fire of Divine Love. Also known as the Pentatauch, The Old Testament.

One who whitens clothes/garments by washing them, hence applied to the disciples/apostles/companions of Jesus (see "hawariyyun” in ) because their trade was apparently to do this. # خمد
To subside, be utterly extinguished (a fire); it's flaming or blazing ceasing,faint or swoon, be allayed (fever or vehemence of a fever), be still/motionless and silent (AI- Qamoos  &  Tajul-Arus), dispose and submit oneself to an affair or event, be silent and dead,become like extinguished ashes.

# خمر
cover/conceal/veil, becoming changed/altered from a former state/condition,mixed/mingled/incorporated/blended, intoxication/wine/grapes, make-up, any intoxicating thing that clouds/obscures the intellect, come upon secretly/unexpectedly, crowding of people, odour of perfume, women's head cover, man's turban, something fermented/matured. khamr n.fem. khumur n.masc. pl. = and let them draw/set/cast (walyadribna) their veils/covers (bikhumurihinna) over (AAala) their breasts/bosom (juyoobihinna).  Lane gives some examples: “the dwelling was hidden by the trees”, “he concealed his testimony”, "She (a woman) applied KhaMaRat as a linament, to beautify her complexion.". "KhiMaR (A woman's muffler, or veil with which she covers her head and the lower part of her face, leaving exposed only the eyes and part or whole of the nose, such is the KhMR worn in the present day: a kind of veil which in Turkish is called Yashmak) a woman's head-covering." 
- bikhumurihinna
- KhaMaRat
- khamr
- khumur

# راي
to see/think/hold, in opinion of, perceive, judge, consider, know. Ara'itaka / ara'itakum = do tell me thou/you (the personal pronoun Kaf is added for emphasis and is not purely as pleonism without adding to the meaning). Tara'ni is for a compound word of a verb tara followed by a pronoun ni meaning you see me. Badi al-ra'yi = having superficial view, outward appearance, first thought, apparently, without proper consideration. Ra'yal'ain = to see with naked eye, judgement by sight. Ri'yun = outward appearance. Ru'ya = vision dream. A'lam tara = behold! lo!Ri'aun = hypocrisy, ostentation, to be seen. tara'a = to see one another, consider, come in sight of one another. yura'una = they deceive hypocritically assuming a false appearance.

# رحم
Rahima = He favored, benefited, pardoned, or forgave him. To love, have tenderness, mercy,pity, forgiveness, have all that is required for exercising beneficence. Tarhamu = He had mercy, pity, or compassion on him; he pitied or compassionated him much. Arham = Wombs (singular) womb, i.e. place of origin. The receptacle of the young in the belly. Ruhmun = Relationship, i.e. nearness of kin, connection by birth; relationship connecting with an ancestor. A connection or tie of relationship. Rahim = it is in the measure of fa'il which denotes the idea of constant repetition and giving.

# زين
To adorn, deck. Adorn, grace, honor {said of an action, quality, or saying}. Embellished, dressed, or trimmed it {relating to language}. Adorned {ex. The earth, or land,became adorned with or by it's herbage}, ornamented, decorated, decked, bedecked, garnished,embellished, beautified, or graced him/it. Of language it is said: "It was embellished, dressed up,or trimmed". Of action it is said: "It was embellished, dressed up"; i.e. commended to a person by another man. A grace, a beauty, a comely quality, a physical/intellectual adornment, an honour or a credit, and anything that is the pride or glory of a person or a thing. A thing that does not disgrace or render unseemly a wise man in any of his states or conditions, either in the present world or in that which is to come. It {states} are of three kinds: Mental: Such as knowledge/science and good tenants. Bodily: Strength, tallness of stature, beauty of aspect. Extrinsic: Wealth, rank or station, dignity.

To deceive, delude, beguile, or outwit. The producing what is false in the form of truth. Embellishment by falsification and deceit. A kind of enchantment, or fascination which captivates the eye and the like. Skillful eloquence [something which is praiseworthy]. Early in the morning, or the first part of day (the time of), a little before daybreak, or the false dawn. Whiteness overspreading blackness. Food marred or spoilt. # سيب
to wander at random, be set free to pasture. saibatun (from saba) = it ran by itself and wandered at random and was set free to pasture. It signifies any domestic beast left to pasture without attention. Liberation of certain domestic animals to pasture and prohibiting their use or slaughter in honour of idols was a practice the Arabs in premeansIslamic days. They were selected mainly on the basis of the number, sex and sequence of their springs. According to some the animal set free was to be the mother of a bahirah an animal which had brought forth females at successive births.

# طه
Mysterious Letter groups of quran. Recite the whole surah keeping these letters in mind througout. You will get an idea why it starts with these letters. It's up to you to decode. According to some sources it is a meaningful expression on its own signifying "O man!" (found in Abyssinian/Syriac/Nabathaean) synonymously "Ya rajulu", in dialect of Akks,an old Arab tribe, it means "Ya habibi" (O my beloved! O great man! Zalima = To be dark, obscure. To give shade {shelter, protection}. A thing that covers, protects,{or shades} one, overhead. zulumati/in/un = Obscurity, darkness. azlama/u = He becomes dark, enters upon the darkness.zulumati/u = Obscurity, darkness. Zalama = To do wrong or evil, treat unjustly, ill-treat, oppress, harm, suppress, tyrannize, misuse,act wrongfully, deprive anyone of a right, misplace, injure, be oppressive, be guilty of injustice,act wickedly, be wanting in or fail. zalimeena = Wrongdoers. zalamtum = You wronged. zalamna = We wronged. zalimun = One who treats unjustly. zalamtu = I wronged, did wrong. yazlimoona/yuzlamoona = They wronged, they shall be wronged. yazlima = He was to do wrong. lam tazlimu/tuzlamu = Stinted not, failing not; you were wronged. yazlima = He was to do wrong. zalimoon = Wrongdoers. mazlooman = Oppressed, vexed, wronged. zulmin = Wrongdoing. zalimoona = Wrongdoers. zalama/thulima/thulma = He wronged/He was wronged. zathlim/u = He does wrong.  zalimeena = Wrongdoers.  tazlimoo = O you! Wrong not  azlama/u = To do unjustly, evil.

# عبد

Li Ya'buduuni = That they worship me. The final ni is a short form of nii of pronominal and not a na plu. Ta'budu = Thou venerate. Ta'buduuna = You worship. A'budu = I worship. Na budu = We serve. I'bud = Thou worship. Tbudu = You serve. Laa ta'bud = Thou adore not. La Ta'buduu = You serve not. Yu'baduuna = They are to be worshipped. Abbadta = Thou enslaved, subjugated. Abdun / Abdan/ Abdin = Slave, Bondman; Servant. Abdaini = Two bond men. Abiduun / Abidiin = Worshippers. Aabidaat = (Women) worshippers. Ibaadat = servitude/worship.

to do/make/act/work/operate/perform/construct/manufacture, practice a handcraft, be active, one who does. amila = he did, acted, worked. amilat = she did, acted. amiluu = they did. amiltum = you did. most often the prefect past tense of this root amila,-s is preceded by man or ma or min of relative or demonstrative pronouns, then it means, "who does",instead of its real meaning of past tense, "who did". yamala = does; did. tamalu = does. this form is used, as a general rule of arabic grammar, to denote the meaning of plural by placing it before the subject. amala = i do. yamaluuna = they do. tamaluuna = you do. namalu/namala/namal = we do. imal = thou do, make, work. imaluu = you do, make, work. amalun /amalan = deed; action; work. amaal = deeds. aamilun = worker; doer. aamiluun / amiliin = workers; doers. aamilatun = toil-worn woman.

# عنق
to be long-necked, become thin in the neck. taanaqa = to embrace. unuqun (pl. anaq) = neck, company (of men), trunk (of a tree), stalk (of a leaf or fruit), heads or chiefs of men,great ones. In it is used as a metaphorical phrase to mean: do not keep your hand stackled to your neck out of miserliness, do not be niggardly. In verse unuqun is metaphorically used and refers to the principle that every action produces an effect which is "made to cling to a person" and that his deeds will be recorded in a Book and that their effect will be seen on the day of resurrection. "Clinging to the neck" indicates the inseparability of one thing from another, thus establishing the law of cause and effect. 
- unuq(pl. anaaq)
- taanaqa 
- unuqun(pl. anaq)

# عقب
to succeed, take the place of, come after, strike on the heel, come at the heel,follow anyone closely. aqqaba = to endeavour repeatedly, return, punish, requitt, retrace one's step. aqab = to die, leave offsprings, give in exchange. aqabatun = place hard to ascent. uqbun = success. taaqqaba = to take careful information, shout, follow step by step. aqub = heel, son,grandson, offspring, pivot, axis. uqba = requital, result, reward, end, success. iqab (pl. aqubat) = punishment after sin, one who puts off or reverses, who looks at the consequence or result of the affair. muaqqibat = who succeed each other, some thing that comes immediately after another thing or succeeds another thing without interruption. it is a double plural feminine of muaqqib. the plural feminine form indicates the frequency of the deeds, since in arabic the feminine form is sometimes employed to impart emphasis and frequency. yuaqqib = look back. aqaba = he retaliated. aagabtum = you punished. aqibuu = you punish. aqaba = caused to follow. uqiba = he was punished; was made to suffer. ugqibtum = you have been persecuted. aqaba = he has punished. uqbun/ugban = result; final end. aqibun = posterior; heel. aqibai = two heels. aqaab = heels. iqaabun = retribution (chastisement) that comes as a result of consequences of sins. aqabatu = steep and difficult ascent; mountain road; road in the upper part of a mountain or a long mountain that lies across the way; difficult affair and path of duty. uqbaa = ending. it is with final yaa, but if added to a pronoun the final yaa turns to alif as uqbahaa = here an alif before haa. aqibatun = end. al-aaqibatu = the happy and good end. muaqqibun = who can reverse. muaqqibaat = those who join their duties in succession; successively ranged.

# عرف
he knew it, had cognition of it, to discern, became acquainted with it, perceiving a thing by reflection and by consideration of the effect, he requited, to acknowledge a part,manager/orderer/overseer, become submissive/tractable/pleasant, the making to know, fragrant,to inform oneself, learn/discover, seek/desire knowledge, benefaction/goodness, mane (of a horse) waves (of the sea), elevated place/portion, higher/highest, first/foremost, a question or questioning respecting a subject of information in order to know it, commonly received/known,to confess/acknowledge/indicate, high mountain, mount arafat. the difference between arafa  &  alima is that the former refers to distinct and specific knowledge,while the latter is more general. opposite to arafa is ankara (to deny) and opposite ot alima is jahila (to be ignorant). alaraf = the elevated place, high dignity, distinguished position, place of discernment or acknowledgement, highest or most elevated faculties of discernment or marifah (knowledge of right and wrong). maruf = honourable, known, recognised, good, befitting, fairness, kindness, custom of society,usage. arafaat (pl. of arafat, masculine araf, being a version of aarif, the active form of arafa and/or arfun, the informational noun of arafa) = means familiarizations, approbations, recognitions.’ arafa = he recognized, acknowledged. arafuu = they recognized. arafta = thou knew. tarifu = thou recognize. yarifuuna = they recognize.  tarifanna = thou should surely recognize. tarifuuna = you shall recognize. yurafu = he is recognized. yurafna = they (f) are/will be recognized. arrafa = made known. taaarafuu = you know each other, recognize each other, do good to each other. yataaarafuuna = they kindness, equity, according to usage, custom of the society, courteous,right. maruufatun = recognized. urfun = seemliness, good. urfan = beneficence, goodness, kindness.

Anything which is veiled or conceals by reason of disdainful pride, or shame of prudency; anything of which one is ashamed when it appears. This root also refers to the pudendum {or external genital organs} of a human being because it is abominable to uncover and look at them. The parts or part of a person which is indecent to expose. Now this is relative because for the slave women it will be indecent to expose what is between the navel and the knee and of the free women it will be indecent to expose everything except the hands and face. # يغوث
Yaguth is name of an idol of the Pagan Arabs. The tribe of Murad was its worshipper. It was in the shape of a lion. M. Asad: As is evident from early sources, these five gods were among the many worshipped by the pre-Islamic Arabs as well (see the book Kitab al-Asnam by Hisham ibn Muhammad al-Kalbi,ed. Ahmad Zaki, Cairo, 1914) Their cult had probably been introduced into Arabia from Syria and Babylonia, where it seems to have existed in early antiquity.  Part of the root i.e. غوث means cry for aid, succor, desiring aid; traveling provision; food to give aid.
- يَغُوثَ

Everything that the male of the form fa'lun (being the representation of the Cl. Arabic form in question, universally applied to all roots) can mean, so can the female (fa'latun or modernly and colloquially fa'lah), with the added meaning of emphasis that the feminization of the word gives; where it is clear that there could be intensification. # لمم
We need to be carefull here. The root لمم gives rise to one particle  لَّمَّا and two other words namely لَمَمَ and لَّمًّا (pay attention to Tanween Fatha). The particle has been dealt separately; check Surah 86 verse 4 etc. Now, لَمَمَ means small faults and لَّمًّا means "together, collect, amass, assemble, pick up greedily, lesser offences, unwilled offences, inadvertent sins etyc."

# ميد
To be shaken, moved, agitated, spread, give food. It was/became in a state of motion or commotion. Was/Became agitated: in a state or violent motion or commotion; or violently agitated. Turn/Twist about. Become contorted and convulsed. Quivered, trembled. Confound, perplex, amazed. Inclining from side to side. Confer bestow benefit/s or favor/s. Maidatan = A table with food upon it. From m-y-d "it was in a state of motion" as the table moved about from what was on it. Or m-y-d "he brought wheat or food" since food is brought on it. Or m-y-d "he gave" because what is this called given by the owner to the people {who are to eat}. Table spread, table with food upon it, food. Knowledge, because knowledge is the spiritual food.

# نبا
(root of nabi)  to be high, lofty, become exalted/elevated, to aquaint/inform, utter in a low voice/sound, cry, bark, gift of prophecy, prophet. To come/go forth, to assault/come upon, went forth, brought/produce. Information; a piece of information; intellegence; an announcement; news; tidings; a piece of news; an account; a narrative, or narration; a story (something that is related from one to another). An announcement of great utility, from which results either knowledge or a predominance of opinion, and true. Eminence/protuberence in the ground/earth. An evident/clear way. Bull, a torrent that comes forth from another land, a man coming forth unexpectedly from an unknown quarter. naba  (pl. of anba) = news/tiding/story/tale. nabiy  (pl. nabiyun and anbiya) = prophet, the prophet (al-nabiy). nubuwah  = prophecy/prophethood. nabbaa = to tell. anbaa = to tell. istanbaa = to ask or be told

# عوق
name of an idol worshipped before the Flood, and then by the Pagan Arabs. It was in the shape of a horse. Banu Hamadan were its worshippers. M. Asad: As is evident from early sources, these five gods were among the many worshipped by the pre-Islamic Arabs as well (see the book Kitab al-Asnam by Hisham ibn Muhammad al-Kalbi,ed. Ahmad Zaki, Cairo, 1914) Their cult had probably been introduced into Arabia from Syria and Babylonia, where it seems to have existed in early antiquity.

# يسر
become gentle/tractable/manageable/easy/submissive/liberal, become little in quantity/paltry / no weight or worth, come from left, divide into parts/portions, make easy/smooth, adapt/accommodate, play with gaming arrows (al maysiri), a game for stakes/wagers involving risk. For those who always wanted to know what "arrows of chance / gaming arrows" were. Lane gives quite a detailed description of what was involved. I'll explain my take on it: a group of 10 (or however many there are) buys an animal on credit (e.g. camel), they slaughter the animal and divide it into 10 portions (equaling the number of people in the group). They assign a number to each portion of meat, e.g. portion | is marked 1, portion 2 is marked 2, portion 3 is marked 3 etc up to 10. They take 10 arrow heads (no feathers) and mark each arrow head with a number,arrow head | marked 1 (this corresponds to meat portion 1), arrow head 2 marked 2 (this corresponds to meat portion 2) etc up to 7, arrows 8, 9 and 10 are left with no mark. They assign a person to shuffle the arrow heads and distribute them to the 10 players. If someone gets arrow 1 they take meat portion 1, if someone gets arrow 2 they take meat portion 2 and so on. Whoever gets the unmarked arrows (3 people in this example) have to pay the entire value of the slaughtered animal, that was initially bought on credit.

# ذوو
is a classical Arabic noun functioning as a possessive particle meaning "possessor of" or "owner of," often used in iḍāfa (construct phrases). It behaves grammatically like an adjective and changes form according to case and gender. In the masculine singular nominative, it is ذُو; in the accusative, ذَا; and in the genitive, ذِي. The dual form appears as ذَوَا (nominative) and ذَوَيْ (accusative/genitive). The plural masculine is ذَوُو or ذَوِي (depending on case), and the plural feminine form is ذَوَاتُ (nominative), ذَوَاتِ (genitive), or ذَوَاتَ (accusative). Feminine singular is ذَاتُ (nominative), ذَاتِ (genitive), or ذَاتَ (accusative). These forms are often prefixed by particles: وَذُو ("and possessor of"), فَذُو ("then possessor of"), لَذُو ("surely possessor of"), وَلِذِي ("and for the one who has"), لِذِي ("for the possessor of"), وَبِذِي ("and with the possessor of"), بِذَاتِ ("with the essence of" or "with the one possessing..."), and وَذَاتَ ("and [feminine] possessor of"). In vocative expressions, يَاذَا ("O possessor of") is used, especially in formal or poetic address. These forms appear throughout the Qur’an and classical literature to indicate traits, ownership, or qualities. For example, وَذُو الْعَرْشِ الْمَجِيدُ ("Owner of the Glorious Throne") and ذِي الطُّولِ ("Possessor of might").
# اول
To return, be before, come back to. To interpret (by consideration), explain. To contract, withdraw, become. To preside over, hold command or authority. To come to be. To put in a proper state/condition, or to compose (an affair). To resort to; namely (a thing of any kind; the thing or place whence he or it originated, or came. His or its origin or source; his or its original state, condition, quantity, weight; any place. A former action, saying, or the like). To return or restore to ( a thing, place, or disposition). To explain, expound, interpret (collecting the meanings of dubious expressions by such expression as is clear, or plain, without dubiousness). To explain the meaning of that which is equivocal or ambiguous. To be seen from a distance. 
- al
- awwal(f. ula)
- awwalun (pl. of awwal)
- awwala

to company, associate, be the friend of or companion to. saahibun (pl. sahbuun and ashaabun) = companion, associate, possessor of any quality or thing, one in an intimate relation with anything, fellow and showing any type of connection or link, helper. saahibatun = spouse,consort, wife. saahaba = to bear company. ashaba = to preserve, hinder, keep from, defend from. yushabuun = they will be accompanied.

//PRONOUN
////DETTACHED PRONOUN
# أَنَا
first-person singular pronoun in Arabic, meaning "I" or "me", used when the speaker refers to themselves. It is a personal pronoun, not derived from a triliteral root, and is essential in both classical and modern Arabic. It has no gender distinction and is used by both males and females. It corresponds to نَحْنُ for the first-person plural ("we"). While أَنَا can appear alone, it is often found in combination with particles that modify its meaning or add emphasis: وَأَنَا ("and I"), فَأَنَا ("then I"), لَأَنَا ("indeed I"), إِنِّي or إِنَّنِي (contracted forms meaning "indeed I"), and مَا أَنَا ("I am not"). These modified forms are frequently used in the Qur’an and classical Arabic to provide emphasis, contrast, or clarify the speaker’s position. For example, إِنِّي أَنَا رَبُّكَ ("Indeed, I am your Lord") and وَأَنَا خَيْرٌ مِّنْهُ ("And I am better than him"). 
# هُوَ
huwa = He.
- huwa
# هُمْ
The forms of this pronoun are هُمُ and هُمْ. هُمُ is used if it comes before definititive article e.g. هُمُ الكتاب. Both means they/themeselves.
-hum
# هِيَ
is the third-person feminine singular pronoun in Arabic, meaning "she" or "it" (when referring to feminine nouns). It is a personal pronoun, not derived from a triliteral root, and is fundamental in both classical and modern Arabic. هِيَ corresponds to هُمْ (they, masculine), هُنَّ (they, feminine), هُوَ (he), and هُمَا (dual). It is often found in various constructions that include conjunctions or particles, which provide emphasis, contrast, or sequence. Common modified forms include فَهِيَ ("then she/it"), وَهِيَ ("and she/it"), and لَهِيَ ("indeed she/it" or "surely she/it"). 
# أَنْتَ
second-person masculine singular pronoun in Arabic, meaning "you" when addressing one male. It is a personal pronoun, not derived from a triliteral root, and is fundamental in both classical and modern Arabic. It corresponds to أَنْتِ for the feminine singular, أَنْتُمَا for dual (both genders), أَنْتُمْ for plural masculine (or mixed), and أَنْتُنَّ for plural feminine. Though it stands independently, أَنْتَ often appears prefixed with particles that modify its meaning: وَأَنْتَ ("and you"), فَأَنْتَ ("then you"), لَأَنْتَ ("surely you"), ءَأَنْتَ ("is it you?"), and أَفَأَنْتَ ("then is it you?"). Additional modified forms include ثُمَّ أَنْتَ ("then you [after something]"), بَلْ أَنْتَ ("rather, you"), and إِنَّكَ أَنْتَ ("indeed, you [emphasized]"), often used for rhetorical emphasis or contrast.
# نَحْنُ
We. 1st person royal plural.
# هما
They both.
-huma	
# هُنّ
is the third-person feminine plural pronoun in Arabic, meaning "they" or "them" when referring exclusively to a group of females. It is a personal, independent pronoun used primarily as a subject in nominal sentences (e.g., هُنَّ مُؤْمِنَاتٌ – "They are believers") or to emphasize the subject of a verbal sentence. Unlike verb-attached object pronouns, هُنَّ stands alone and is not derived from a triliteral root—it is an essential part of the Arabic pronominal system. The form is fixed and indeclinable. In Classical Arabic and the Qur’an, هُنَّ often appears with conjunctions like وَهُنَّ ("and they"), or in emphatic contexts. It contrasts with هُم (for males or mixed groups) and corresponds to other feminine pronouns like هِيَ (she), أَنْتُنَّ (you all, feminine), and هُنَّ also appears in grammatical agreement, requiring verbs, adjectives, and participles to reflect feminine plural endings (e.g., فَعَلْنَ for verbs). It is an integral part of clear gender distinction in Arabic grammar.
////DEMONSTRATIVE PRONOUN
# هَـٰذَا
this. pointing, indicating, or demonstrating something clearly of near. The two forms used are هَـٰذَا (this - masc.) and هَـٰذِهِ (this - fem.). These are used for near demonstration while the distant counterparts are ذَلِكَ and تِلْكَ.
# ذَٰلِكَ
that. pointing, indicating, or demonstrating something clearly of distant. Forms are ذَلِكَ, ذَلِكُنَّ, ذَانِكَ, ذَلِكُمْ.
# تِلْكَ
This; that.
- tilka 
# أُو۟لَـٰٓئِكَ
The words أُو۟لَـٰٓئِكَ  and هَـٰٓؤُلَآءِ usually mean these/those. أُو۟لَـٰٓئِكُمْ is a form of أُو۟لَـٰٓئِكَ and أُو۟لَآءِ is a form of هَـٰٓؤُلَآءِ. أُو۟لَـٰٓئِكَ usually used fot distant things hence  usually means those. هَـٰٓؤُلَآءِ is usually used for near object and thence usually means these.
# هَـٰٓؤُلَآءِ
The words أُو۟لَـٰٓئِكَ  and هَـٰٓؤُلَآءِ usually mean these/those. أُو۟لَـٰٓئِكُمْ is a form of أُو۟لَـٰٓئِكَ and أُو۟لَآءِ is a form of هَـٰٓؤُلَآءِ. أُو۟لَـٰٓئِكَ usually used fot distant things hence  usually means those. هَـٰٓؤُلَآءِ is usually used for near object and thence usually means these.
////RELATIVE PRONOUN
# الَّذِي
The three words الَّذِي, الَّذِينَ, الَّتِي  are RELATIVE PRONOUNS and are used as conjunction, meaning 'that, which, who, whom'. They serve the purpose of joining nouns/pronouns or verbs to other nouns or verbs. They have a definite forms e.g. الَّذِي is used as singular male (one who/he who/of whom/of which). الَّذِينَ is used as plural male (those who/of those/they who).  The female singular counterpart is الَّتِي (she who/of whom/of which). The female plural form (اللَّاتِي or اللَّوَاتِي) is not used in Quran.
# الَّتِي
The three words الَّذِي, الَّذِينَ, الَّتِي  are RELATIVE PRONOUNS and are used as conjunction, meaning 'that, which, who, whom'. They serve the purpose of joining nouns/pronouns or verbs to other nouns or verbs. They have a definite forms e.g. الَّذِي is used as singular male (one who/he who/of whom/of which). الَّذِينَ is used as plural male (those who/of those/they who).  The female singular counterpart is الَّتِي (she who/of whom/of which). The female plural form (اللَّاتِي or اللَّوَاتِي) is not used in Quran.
//PARTICLE
////INSEPARABLE_PREPOSITION
# مَعَ
The root (م ع) conveys the idea of accompaniment, association, togetherness, and proximity. It appears in various forms in Arabic, often relating to being with, accompanying, or association. مَعَ (ma‘a) = With, together with, alongside. مَعِيَّة (ma‘iyyah) = Companionship, being together, presence. مَعَهُ (ma‘ahu) = With him. مَعَهُمْ (ma‘ahum) = With them. مَعِي (ma‘ī) = With me. مُعَانَاة (mu‘ānāh) = Endurance, suffering, struggling with something. مُعَاوَنَة (mu‘āwanah) = Assistance, cooperation, helping one another. مُعَاهَدَة (mu‘āhadah) = Treaty, agreement, pact between parties. مُعَايَشَة (mu‘āyashah) = Coexistence, living together in harmony.
# عِندَ
It means here, with, by, at the point of, about, from, in the presence of. The word denotes the idea of nearness, whether it be actual in the sense of possession or ideational, it also denotes a sense of rank or dignity or opinion, time and place. The root عند forms different words, you can check 11:59.
# بِ
{PREFIX}, {PREPOSITION}An inseperable preposition: With the help of, During, For, By, On account of, According to,In, From. "Bismillah = with the name/assistance/help of The God/Divine/Deity" 
////SEPARABLE_PREPOSITION
# مِنْ
Separable preposition. مِنْ is a particle but مَنْ is an iterogative pronoun. مِنْ means of, from, against, before. If hum/huma is added as suffix then "from/of them/those/both of them e.g. مِنْهَا, مِنْهُمْ. If la is prefixed (لَمِنَ) means surely among. if kum(مِنْكُمْ) is suffixed then means from/of you. if nay(مِنِّي) is suffixed then it means "from/of me" . مَنْ means  whoever/whichever. مَنْ can also mean who?. The particle مِمَّنْ is combination of مِنْ (from) مَنْ (whoever) usually means from whom.
# فِى
Separable preposition. which points to cause, space or time. In, into, amongst, in company with, during, with, of, for, for the sake of, upon, concerning, after, in comparison, about, among,on account of, respecting, by the side of, on, by, against, according to, in respect of. 
# عَنْ
Separable preposition. "from," "about," or "concerning," often used to indicate separation, source, or detachment.  When followed by pronouns, it attaches directly and changes form accordingly: عَنْهُ ("from him"), عَنْهَا ("from her"), عَنْهُمْ ("from them"), عَنْهُمَا ("from both of them"), عَنْكَ ("from you," masculine), عَنْكُمْ or emphatically عَنْكُمُ ("from you all"), عَنِّي or عَنِّي ("from me"), and عَنَّا ("from us"). It also appears with conjunctions such as وَعَنْ ("and from") and وَعَنِ ("and from," with phonetic adjustment before sun letters). Appears in expressions that convey rejection, removal, or discourse—such as turning away from something or someone, or speaking about a matter. For example, عَنْهُمْ appears in the phrase فَأَعْرِضْ عَنْهُمْ ("So turn away from them"), and عَنِّي in فَصَفَحْتُ عَنْكُمْ ("I forgave you").
# عَلَىٰ
Separable preposition. on, upon, at, under, against, provided, so that, in respect,before, against, according to, for the sake of, to, above, inspite of, near. (e.g. ala hudan = they are on guidance).
# رُبَمَا
perhaps.
# حَتَّى
until, unless, till, for. 
# الي
to, at.
# تَحْتَ
(taht)  Particle: that which is below; the lower part; beneath; inferior, slop; declivity of a mountain; under. It is the opposite of fawg/above. This particle has been used about 51 times in al quran. 
////NOUNS_USED_AS_PREPOSITION
# لَدُنْ
a preposition meaning "from" or "in the presence of", conveying a sense of closeness, nearness, or directness of origin. It is often used with pronoun suffixes to indicate possession or relationship with a person or source. Unlike its more general counterpart عِندَ, which also means "with" or "at", لَدُنْ is more elevated and emphatic, frequently appearing in Qur’anic and literary contexts. For example: عِلْمًا مِّن لَّدُنَّا — "knowledge from Us (directly from Us)." لَدُنْكَ = "from you", لَدُنْهُ = "from him", لَدُنَّا = "from Us", لَدُنِّي = "from me". These forms attach pronoun suffixes to convey personalized direction or source. The structure emphasizes intimacy or privilege, often suggesting divine origin or special closeness in spiritual or formal usage. For instance, in وَآتَيْنَاهُ رَحْمَةً مِّن لَّدُنَّا — "And We gave him mercy from Us", the use of لَدُنَّا implies a special, chosen mercy directly from Allah. The base لَدُنْ is indeclinable and not derived from a typical triliteral root, making it unique in its structure and use.
# لَدَيْ
at; near; with; from; presence; in the presence of; for. ladun = particle of a place or preposition. ladai/lida =  at; near; with; from; presence; in the presence of; for. a {particle} of place or  {preposition}. the meanings of ladun and ladai and lida are the same. min ladun = that which proceeds from the presence of, of one's part, from before (often translated as "from"). 
- ladun   
- ladai/lida
- min ladun
////PARTCILES_OF_CO-ORDINATION
# ثَمَّ
over there,here, in that direction, there in, thither. The root ثمم may mean to pick up, collect, repair, heap up (thing in a place) but not used in Quran.
# ثُمَّ
then, afterwards, moreover, mostly used as a conjunction indicating a sequence in line or order to be rendered as then/thereafter, also used as a more simple conjunction like "and", "e.g.". The root ثمم may mean to pick up, collect, repair, heap up (thing in a place) but not used in Quran.
# أَمْ
In Arabic, the word **"أَمْ"** is a conjunction primarily used in interrogative sentences to present a choice between two options, similar to the English word "or" in questions. For example, in the sentence **"أزيدٌ في المنزل أم عمرو؟"** (*Is Zaid at home, or is it Amr?*), "أَمْ" is used to offer a choice between two possibilities. It can also serve a rhetorical function, meaning **"or rather"**, to indicate a shift or correction in thought, such as in **"سأذهب إلى السوق أم إلى المكتبة؟"** (*Shall I go to the market or the library?*). On the other hand, **"أَمِ"** is a much less common variant, sometimes appearing in poetry or dialects as a phonetic alteration of "أَمْ." However, it is not frequently used in formal Arabic writing or speech.
# او
or, unless. أَوْ and أَوِ.
# بَلْ
an indeclinable particle (حرف إضراب) in Arabic used to indicate transition, correction, or contrast between two statements. It is typically translated as "rather," "instead," or "on the contrary." It negates or replaces what comes before it, introducing a more accurate or emphasized idea. For example: مَا هُوَ شَاعِرٌ بَلْ هُوَ نَبِيٌّ — "He is not a poet; rather, he is a prophet." It is frequently used in rhetorical speech and the Qur’an to refine or reject a prior assumption or statement. The form بَلِ is a phonetic variant of بَلْ, where the final consonant gets a kasrah (ـِ) when followed by a consonant-starting noun and for phonological smoothness, especially in poetic or rhythmic contexts. It does not change the meaning — it's simply for ease of pronunciation (known as الوصل الصوتي).
# لَـٰكِن
{PARTICLE} = but (after a negation); but not (after an affirmation). 
////PARTCILES OF EMPHASIS
# انن
that, indeed. إِنَّ	= Indeed, إِنَّهُمْ =	indeed they, إِنَّهَا	Indeed it, إِنَّهُ = Indeed he, إِنَّا	= Indeed We, إِنِّي = Indeed I,    إِنَّا = Indeed we, إِنَّكَ= Indeed you, أَنَّ = that, أَنَّهُمْ = that they, أَنَّهُ	= he, إِنِّي = that I (am), إِنَّكَ	= Indeed you, أَئِنَّكُمْ	= Do you truly, أَنَّكُمْ	= that you etc.
# إِنَّمَا
only. فَإِنَّمَا = then/so only. Derived from this is the word أَنَّمَا = that only, that which/what, that.
# لَعَلَّ
particle of hope and expectation used to indicate possibility, hope, or likelihood. May be treated as having a root لعل (which means Perhaps; may be that; it is hoped; to be happy. It is used to denote either a state of hope or fear, whether that state pertains to the speaker or to the addressee or to someone else, expectation). It is often translated as "perhaps," "so that," "that one may," or "it is hoped that. "لَعَلَّكُمْ = "so that you may" / "perhaps you may". لَعَلَّهُمْ = "so that they may" / "perhaps they may". لَعَلِّي = "perhaps I" / "that I may". لَعَلَّهُ = "perhaps he" / "that he may". لَعَلَّكَ = "perhaps you" / "so that you may". لَعَلَّنَا = "that we may"
# قَدْ
a particle in Arabic that carries different meanings based on context and the verb that follows. When used before a past tense verb (فعل ماضٍ), it indicates certainty or emphasis, often translated as "indeed" or "surely." For example, قَدْ أَفْلَحَ الْمُؤْمِنُونَ — "Indeed, the believers have succeeded." When followed by a present tense verb (فعل مضارع), it expresses possibility or occasionally, translated as "might," "may," or "perhaps" — e.g., قَدْ يَفْعَلُ — "He might do." لَقَدْ = "Surely" / "Certainly", combining لام التوكيد (the particle لَـ for emphasis) with قَدْ. Example: لَقَدْ خَلَقْنَا الإِنسَانَ — "Indeed, We have created man." وَقَدْ = "And indeed", linking two statements. فَقَدْ = "Then indeed" / "So surely", showing consequence or emphasis. وَلَقَدْ = "And surely", frequently used at the beginning of verses in the Qur'an for narrative flow and strong emphasis. قَدِ and لَقَدِ are rare variants where قَدْ carries a kasrah (i.e., قَدِ) due to phonetic or poetic reasons, especially in classical texts or pre-Qur’anic poetry.
# كَأَنَّمَا
compound particle in Arabic that functions to express similitude or approximation, often translated as "as if" or "it is as though." It combines كَأَنَّ (which means "as if") with مَا (a particle that can function in various ways, here adding emphasis or abstraction). The expression كَأَنَّمَا is used to introduce hypothetical, metaphorical, or imagined scenarios, often for illustrative or rhetorical effect. For example: كَأَنَّمَا يُسَاقُونَ إِلَى الْمَوْتِ — "As if they are being driven to death." In many contexts, it appears prefixed with فَـ (fa) as فَكَأَنَّمَا, meaning "then it is as if" or "so it is as though," especially to follow a cause-and-effect or narrative flow. This form is frequently used in the Qur’an and classical Arabic to deepen emotional or rhetorical impact. For example: فَكَأَنَّمَا قَتَلَ النَّاسَ جَمِيعًا — "Then it is as if he had killed all of mankind." (Surah Al-Ma'idah 5:32) Grammatically, كَأَنَّ is one of the sisters of إنَّ (أخوات إنَّ) and introduces a nominal sentence where the subject is in the accusative case. مَا in كَأَنَّمَا is not always treated as an independent word and often acts as part of the whole expression.
# وَيْكَأَنَّ
a rare and expressive compound particle in Classical Arabic, found primarily in the Qur’an and early poetry. It is composed of وَيْ, an interjection of surprise, realization, or wonder, and كَأَنَّ, meaning "as if" or "it is as though." Together, وَيْكَأَنَّ expresses a moment of sudden realization or emphatic reflection, often translated as "Alas! It is as if...", or simply "Oh! Indeed..." e.g. وَيْكَأَنَّهُ لَا يُفْلِحُ الْكَافِرُونَ — "Alas! It is as if the disbelievers will never succeed." (Surah Al-Qasas 28:82). Here, وَيْكَأَنَّهُ combines the emotional interjection with the grammatical function of كَأَنَّ plus هُ (he/it), forming a powerful phrase of realization or awakening. Grammatically, كَأَنَّ remains one of the sisters of إِنَّ, requiring a nominal sentence afterward. The particle وَيْ is fixed and not productive in modern usage, but it adds dramatic flair in classical texts.
# لِ
means "for" or "to". This preposition is then attached to pronoun suffixes to indicate possession or direction toward someone. لَهُ=for him, لَهُمْ=for them (masc.), لَهَا=for her, لَهُمَا=for them two, لَهُنَّ=for them (fem.), لِي=for me, لَنَا=for us, لَكَ=for you (masc. sing.), لَكِ=for you (fem. sing.), لَكُمَا=for you two, لَكُمْ=for you all (masc.), لَكُمُ=for you all (masc.), فَلَهُ=so for him, فَلَهُمْ=so for them (masc.), فَلَهَا=so for her, فَلَهُمَا=so for them two, فَلَهُنَّ=so for them (fem.), فَلَكُمْ=so for you all (masc.), فَلَكُمُ=so for you all (masc.), وَلَهُ=and for him, وَلَهُمْ=and for them (masc.), وَلَهَا=and for her, وَلَهُنَّ=and for them (fem.), وَلَنَا=and for us, وَلِيَ=and for me, وَلَكَ=and for you (masc. sing.), وَلَكُمْ=and for you all (masc.), وَلَكُمُ=and for you all (masc.), وَلَهُمُ=and for them (masc.), لِيَ=for me (emphatic), أَلَهُمْ=is it for them?, أَلَكُمُ=is it for you all?
# ليت
The root means, to give a reply which was not called for, shift, shun a question, conceal, diminish, withold, prevent, deprive. If we consider the particle لَيْتَ then check 89:24.
# لَيْتَ
May it be that? Would that! Particle of desire and expressing a wish impossible to be realized, لَيْتَنِى means I wish. It is one of those particles which like anna require a following noun to be an accusative. This particle has occurred 14 times. The root ليت has occured in Quran in one instance (49:14) as Yalit (يَلِتْكُم) where it means to shun, deprive, prevent etc.
////NEGATIVE_PARTICLE
# لَا
A negation  {PARTICLE}  meaning "no" or "not," used to deny actions or statements, such as لَا أَعْبُدُ مَا تَعْبُدُونَ ("I do not worship what you worship").
# لَمَّا
There are two meanings. It is most commonly used with verbs in the jussive mood (المضارع المجزوم), and it negates an action that hasn't happened yet but is expected to happen. e.g. "He has not come yet". Another meaning is as conjunction "when" e.g. "When Zayd came, I was happy". During usage as negation  particle "not yet" it is almost similar to  لَمْ but there is slight difference. لَمْ means "didn't"  and the state is expected to stay same while in لَّمَّا there is expectation that the state may become affirmative.
# لَن
it is a negative particle meaning "never". However the root لن means to soften, make tender, lenient, gentle, smooth. Example words derived from the root are: linatun = a palm-tree of which the dates are of very inferior quality and are unfit for human consumption. 
- linta
- talinu
- alanna
- layyina
- linatun
# لَيْسَ
It was not; is not. It is one of the verbs of the class of kana which govern the attribute in the accusative.
# غير
bring, convey, benefit, bestowed upon; alter or change a thing for the worse,corrupted, tainted, infected, render it ill-smelling; adjusting, repairing; difference between two persons; become jealous; procure; other than (exclusively), except, without; lies; (a man’s) dislike of another’s participation in what supposedly his right; care of what is sacred or inviolable to avoid suspicion of disdain; cognizance or badge of a free non-Muslim subjects in a Muslim government; one who puts off the furniture of his camel from off him to relieve and ease him. 
- ghayyara
- falaghayyirunna
- yughayyiru
- yughayyiruu
- mughayyiran
- taghayyara
- ghayri
////INTEROGATIVE_PARTICLES
# هَلْ
is an Arabic interrogative particle (أداة استفهام) used to ask yes–no questions, seeking affirmation or denial. It is indeclinable and not derived from a triliteral root. Commonly placed at the beginning of a sentence, it introduces questions such as: هَلْ تَعْلَمُ؟ ("Do you know?") or هَلْ نَزَلَ الْكِتَابُ؟ ("Has the Book been sent down?"). Variations include فَهَلْ ("then, do...?"), which adds a sense of consequence or continuation; وَهَلْ ("and do...?"), which connects to a preceding statement; and هَلِ, a phonetic form used before sun letters for smoother pronunciation (e.g., هَلِ الدِّينُ حَقٌّ؟). These forms occur frequently in the Qur’an and classical Arabic to prompt reflection or emphasize rhetorical questioning, such as فَهَلْ أَنتُم مُّنتَهُونَ؟ ("Will you then desist?") and هَلْ جَزَاءُ الْإِحْسَانِ إِلَّا الْإِحْسَانُ؟ ("Is the reward for good anything but good?").
# مَتَىٰ
an interrogative particle in Arabic that means "when", used to ask about time in a question. It is used with verbs to inquire about the time of an action’s occurrence, and can be translated as: "When will...?," "When did...?," or "At what time...?" depending on the verb tense. For example: مَتَى تَقُومُ السَّاعَةُ؟ — "When will the Hour be established?" It can also function as a conditional particle in certain classical or Qur’anic contexts, where it carries the meaning of "whenever" or "if ever." For instance: مَتَى مَا تَدْعُوهُ يَسْمَعْ لَكُمْ — "Whenever you call upon Him, He hears you." Though often seen at the beginning of a sentence, it can also appear mid-sentence for rhetorical or poetic purposes. It is indeclinable and not derived from a triliteral root, acting as a fixed part of Arabic's interrogative system, alongside others like أَيْنَ (where), كَيْفَ (how), and كَمْ (how many).
# مَنْ
it is an iterogative pronoun. مَنْ means  whoever/whichever. مَنْ can also mean who?. The particle مِمَّنْ is combination of مِنْ (from) مَنْ (whoever) usually means from whom.
# كَمْ
How many, how much.
# مَا
The word ما (formed from م-ا) is a highly versatile particle in Arabic with multiple meanings and grammatical functions. It can serve as a relative pronoun meaning "that which", an interrogative pronoun meaning "what?", a negation particle meaning "not", or an emphasizing particle. In Quranic and classical Arabic, ما appears in different contexts, such as in ما هذا؟ ("What is this?") for questions, ما جاء أحد ("No one came") for negation, and بما أنزل الله ("With what Allah has revealed") as a relative pronoun. Its wide range of uses makes it one of the most fundamental and frequently occurring words in Arabic. The context where it appears is very important to know what it means. Picking a single sentence sometimes may not suffice to derive its meaning. 
# كيف
How (كَيْفَ). 
# لَمْ
negation particle in Arabic, used to negate past tense actions, not.
- لَمْ
- أَلَمْ
- وَلَمْ
- أَوَلَمْ
- فَلَمْ
- فَإِلَّمْ
- أَفَلَمْ
# أَنَّىٰ
an independent interrogative particle (noun, أَسْمَاءُ الاسْتِفْهَام), meaning "how," "from where," or "in what way."  Its usage is often rhetorical or expressive, frequently appearing in contexts of wonder, denial, or astonishment. For example, أَنَّى لَكِ هَذَا؟ ("From where did you get this?") or أَنَّى يُحْيِي هَٰذِهِ ٱللَّهُ بَعْدَ مَوْتِهَا؟ ("How will Allah bring this back to life after its death?"). The word also appears in combination with particles that enhance its rhetorical function: فَأَنَّى ("so how" or "then how"), and وَأَنَّى ("and how"), which are used for logical continuation or emphasis. These forms do not alter its core meaning but enrich its contextual tone. أَنَّى remains fixed in form and case, as is typical of interrogative particles in Arabic.
# أَيْنَ
primarily associated with location and spatial inquiry. One of its most common usages is in the interrogative particle أَيْنَ (ʾayna), which means "where?" and is used to ask about the location of a person or object, such as in the sentence "أَيْنَ تَسْكُنُ؟" (ʾayna taskunu? – "Where do you live?"). From this root, the noun أَيْنِيَّة (ʾayniyya) is derived, which refers to "where-ness" or "spatiality", a term sometimes used in philosophy to discuss the concept of place. Although verbs from this root are rare in classical Arabic, some sources mention forms like أَانَ، يُؤْيِنُ (ʾāna, yuʾīnu), which can mean "to determine a place" or "to localize something", though such usages are not common in everyday language. 
# مَاذَا
The particle مَا if followed by ذَا then it will always be interrogative. So, it will always mean Interrogative مَا mostly mean "what".
# ايي
The root أ-ي-ي gives rise to several words in Arabic, primarily used for specification, selection, emphasis, and addressing. أَيُّ means "which" or "whichever," with its feminine form أَيَّةُ. In vocative expressions, أَيَّهَا (masculine) and أَيَّتُهَا (feminine) are used. The word أَيًّا conveys "whichever" or "any," while أَيَّهُمْ and أَيُّكُمْ mean "which of them" and "which of you," respectively. أَيُّهَ is a variant vocative form, and أَيَّمَا means "whichever" or "whatever." The exclusive pronoun إِيَّا appears in words like إِيَّاكَ ("you alone"), إِيَّايَ ("me alone"), and إِيَّاهُ ("him alone"), emphasizing exclusivity. The plural form إِيَّاكُمْ means "you alone" (plural). Prepositional forms include بِأَيِّ ("by which" or "with which") and لِأَيِّ ("for which"). These words appear frequently in the Quran and classical Arabic, serving functions in questioning, addressing, and exclusivity.
////CONDITION_PARTICLES
# إِذْ
إِذْ and إِذَا are two closely related particles that developed from an obsolete noun meaning 'time'. إِذْ in general as a conjunction means 'when (usually refer to past event)' or 'since'. The only exception to this is when it is used with قَبْلَ or بَعْدَ to form compound conjuctions; قَبْلَ إِذْ (before) and بَعْدَ إِذْ (after, 28:87). إِذْ is most commonly followed by the perfect but imperfect and nominal sentences also occur. In Quran however, إِذْ almost always means 'when'. In the Quran the combination وَإِذْ at the beginning of a verse has a specific and highly idiomatic use to introduce a piece of narrative. The only convincing way that this can be rendered into English is to translate it as 'and (remember) when unless it occurs in a series (e.g. in verses 2:49, 50, 51, 53, 54, 55) when the 'remember' is required only on the first occasion. This translation of وَإِذْ is confirmed by a handful of examples in which the Imperative 'remember' actually occurs e.g. وَإِذْ قَالَ رَبُّكَ لِلْمَلَائِكَةِ إِنِّي خَالِقٌۭ بَشَرًۭا مِّن صَلْصَـٰلٍۢ مِّنْ حَمَإٍۢ مَّسْنُونٍۢ (15:28). إِذِ – A rare form of إِذْ, sometimes used before words starting with ا to ease pronunciation.  
# إِذَا
إِذْ and إِذَا are two closely related particles that developed from an obsolete noun meaning 'time'. إِذَا or إِذًا has two functions: (1) As a conjunction meaning 'when', 'as often as'. إِذَا is normally followed by the Perfect, though the Imperfect is sometimes found. Usually it implies a condition, and then its construction is normally that of a conditional sentence. The fulller form إِذَا مَا is rare. Interestingly, in early Meccan surahs إِذَا is used an exclamatory, almost oath-like way. e.g. Surah 81 and 82. (2) As an interjection if followed by a noun or pronoun meaning 'Lo', 'Behold'. This is less common than conjunction إِذَا and mostly followed by a third person pronoun.

The word إِنْ	and إِينْ means if.	إِنِ	is it's short form. أَنْ	means to or that.	أَنِ	is short form.
# مهما
Whatever.

law {PARTICLE} = if/though/although, conditional. Often at the start of verses/sentences. 
////PARTCILES_OF_EXCEPTION
# دُون
arabic preposition (حرف جر) meaning besides, apart from, beneath, or without. When used in an iḍāfa (construct phrase) or followed by a pronoun, it takes a kasrah (ـِ) to become دُونِ. It connects with various pronouns to form phrases like: دُونِي (other than me), دُونِهِ (other than him), دُونِهَا (other than her), دُونِكَ (other than you, masculine), دُونِكُمْ (other than you all), دُونِنَا (other than us), دُونِهِمْ (other than them), and so on. It is a preposition. It frequently appears in expressions such as مِن دُونِ الله (“besides Allah”) to express exclusion or false reliance on other than God.
# إِلَّا
A particle of exception meaning "except" or "unless," as in لَا إِلٰهَ إِلَّا اللَّهُ ("There is no god except Allah").
////FUTURE_PARTICLE
# سَوْفَ
an indeclinable particle in Arabic used to indicate future tense when placed before a present tense verb, and is often translated as "will" or "shall." It implies that the action will take place in the future, sometimes more distantly than the shorter future particle سَـ. For example: سَوْفَ يُعْطِيكَ رَبُّكَ ("Your Lord will give you"). It frequently appears in prefixed forms to express added meaning or emphasis: فَسَوْفَ ("then will") shows consequence, وَسَوْفَ ("and will") connects to a prior statement, لَسَوْفَ ("surely will") adds certainty through لَام التوكيد, فَلَسَوْفَ ("then surely will") combines consequence and emphasis, and وَلَسَوْفَ ("and surely will") links and affirms strongly. For instance, وَلَسَوْفَ يُعْطِيكَ رَبُّكَ فَتَرْضَىٰ — "And surely your Lord will give you, so you will be pleased." These forms are common in both classical and Qur’anic Arabic for expressing future actions with varying degrees of certainty, emphasis, or logical flow.
////COMPUND_PARTICLES
# عَمَّا
This is a compound particle. This is formed by combination of  عَنْ and مَا giving the meaning of "about what" or "above what" or "from what" or "of what".
# مِمَّا
This is a compound particle. This is formed by combination of  مِنْ and مَا giving the meaning of  "from what" or "of what" or "than what". There are three forms of this combined particle: مِمَّا, مِّمَّا and  مِمَّ. "مِمَّ" is essentially a shortened or contracted form of "مِمَّا" used in poetry or ease of pronunciation.
# لَوْلَا
This is a compound particle. This is formed by combination of لَوْ and لَا. It is used in two main ways: Expressing regret or blame – It means "if only" or "why not" and is used to criticize or encourage an action. Indicating conditionality – It means "if it were not for" and is followed by a noun or pronoun.
# بِمَا
This is a compound particle. This is formed by combination of بِ and  مَا giving meanings like "because of what", "with what" / "by means of what". It also appears in phrases indicating a cause-and-effect relationship, such as بِمَا كُنتُمْ تَعْمَلُونَ ("as you used to do"). بِمَ is essentially a shortened or contracted form of بِمَا used in poetry or ease of pronunciation.
# كَمَا
This is a compound particle. This is formed by combination of كَ and مَا. كَ is a preposition meaning "like" or "as". Together this mean like that, like what or simply as, like,
# مَالِ
This is a compound particle. This is formed by combination of مَا and لِ. If we break down then it may mean "what for/to" but idiomatically it means "What is the matter with...?" or "What is wrong with...?" It expresses surprise, disbelief, or questioning regarding a specific subject. The phrase is followed by a noun or pronoun, as in "مَالِ هَذَا الْكِتَابِ؟" (What is the matter with this book?) from Surah Al-Kahf (18:49).
# لِمَ
The particle مَا if followed by ذَا then it will always be interrogative. Moreover, in many places مَا is prefixed by a preposition like بِ, لِ and فِى and sometimes مَا is shortened to مَ. The particle لِمَ is shortened form of لِمَاذَا. Where ذَا is removed and مَا is shortened. So, لِمَ can only be interrogative. It means "Why?" or "For what reason?"
# لِمَا
This is a compound particle. This is formed by combination of لِ (preposition) and مَا. It means "That/to/for/on which/what/whom?" i.e. it is a relative particle (not interogative).
# لَمَا
This is a compound particle. This is formed by combination of لَ (particle) and مَا. لَ is a particle of emphasis (surely, indeed, certainly). If meaning of مَا is taken whatever, then لَمَا will mean certainly whatever, indeed what etc.
# لَئِنْ
a compound conditional particle in Arabic formed by merging لَ (the emphatic prefix لام التوكيد) with إِنْ (a particle meaning "if" for conditional clauses). Together, لَئِنْ conveys a strongly emphasized conditional statement, typically translated as "surely if" or "verily if." It frequently appears in Qur’anic and classical Arabic to intensify hypothetical or cautionary scenarios. For example: لَئِنْ شَكَرْتُمْ لَأَزِيدَنَّكُمْ — "If you are grateful, I will surely increase you." This structure often pairs لَئِنْ in the "if" clause with a verb beginning with لَـ in the response clause for symmetry and emphasis. Variants like وَلَئِنْ ("and surely if") and وَلَئِنِ appear at the start of verses or sentences to connect and emphasize conditional statements. The form لَئِنِ (with a kasrah) occurs when followed by a noun or for phonetic smoothness. لِأَنْ, while similar in appearance, is actually a combination of لِـ (for/to) and أَنْ (that, to), typically used to express purpose or intent, as in لِأَنْ يُقِيمُوا الصَّلَاةَ — "so that they may establish prayer." Though visually similar, لَئِنْ and لِأَنْ serve different grammatical roles: one conditional, the other purposive.
# أَمَّا
Though it seems to come from root امم (which is dealt separately, check 80:35), it actually is compund particle formed by أَمْ and مَا. أَمْ is a particle used in choices or contrasts. مَا here functions as a connector that emphasizes or supports the topicalization. It introduces a topic, then leads into a comment or judgment about it. Usually translates as: “As for...”, “Regarding...”, “Now, concerning...”. It’s always followed by a فَ (fa') before the response or comment clause.
////AFFIRMATIVE Particle
# بَلَىٰ
بَلَىٰ is a particle but same root بلي is used to mean decay. The particle بَلَىٰ is used in response to a question to affirm a positive answer (used in a context of negation) e.g. Yes!
# نَعَمْ
particle used to confirm or agree. e.g. "yes". It has same root as نعم. 
# اي
The word **"إِي"** is an **affirmative particle** in Arabic used specifically to express strong agreement, particularly in the context of oaths. Unlike **"نَعَم"**, which is a general word for "yes," **"إِي"** is almost exclusively used when affirming a statement with an oath, often followed by **"وَرَبِّي"** (by my Lord) or similar phrases. For example, in the Quran: **"قُلْ إِي وَرَبِّي إِنَّهُ لَحَقٌّ"** (*Say: Yes, by my Lord, it is the truth.*) [Yunus 10:53]. This usage highlights certainty and solemn affirmation. While **"إِي"** is rarely used in modern spoken Arabic, it remains significant in classical Arabic, religious texts, and formal discourse where emphatic affirmation is needed.
////OTHER particles
# هَاؤُم
demonstrative or presentative particle used to draw attention and hand something over emphatically, meaning "Here you are!" or "Take this!" It appears in the Qur'an in Surah Al-Haqqah (69:19): "هَاؤُمُ اقْرَءُوا كِتَابِيَهْ" – "Here, read my book!" Grammatically, it is considered a compound particle formed from هاء التنبيه (the attention-getting "ha") and a form of imperative delivery, addressing a group. 
# كَيْ
is a subordinating particle (حرف مصدري و نصب) in Arabic, meaning "so that" or "in order that," used to express purpose. It is not derived from a triliteral root and serves a purely grammatical function. The verb that follows كَيْ must appear in the subjunctive case (منصوب). Variants include لِكَيْ (a combination of لِـ "for" and كَيْ), meaning "so that"; and لِكَيْلَا, which combines لِـ, كَيْ, and لَا, meaning "so that not" or "in order that not." These forms are frequent in classical Arabic and the Qur’an. For example, كَيْ لَا تَأْسَوْا عَلَى مَا فَاتَكُمْ ("so that you do not grieve over what has escaped you") and لِكَيْلَا يَكُونَ دُولَةً بَيْنَ الْأَغْنِيَاءِ مِنكُمْ ("so that it does not circulate only among the rich among you").
//ADVERBS
# هَـٰكَذَا
is an adverbial expression in Arabic meaning "like this," "thus," or "in this manner." It is formed from the demonstrative prefix ha- (هَـ) and the deictic particle kadhā (كَذَا), which itself carries the meaning "like that." Together, hakadha (هَكَذَا) points to the manner or way in which something is done, typically referring to something close or immediately apparent to the speaker. It is commonly used in both classical and modern Arabic to describe or refer to actions, states, or behaviors. The interrogative form ahakadha (أَهَكَذَا) adds the question particle a- (أَ) at the beginning, turning it into a yes/no question: "Is it like this?" or "Is this how it is?" This form appears in rhetorical contexts, often expressing surprise, disapproval, or emphasis.
# هُنَاكَ
an adverb of place meaning "there", used to indicate a distant or remote location in contrast to هُنَا ("here"). It is commonly used in both classical and modern Arabic to situate events, actions, or beings in a place that is far from the speaker. A more emphatic variant is هُنَالِكَ, which combines هُنَا (here) with the suffix ـلِكَ (to/for that), adding rhetorical force or specificity. هُنَالِكَ is often used to signal a moment of decisive judgment or significant transition, such as هُنَالِكَ الْوَلَايَةُ لِلَّهِ الْحَقِّ ("There, the [true] authority belongs to Allah, the Truth") [18:44]. Both words trace back conceptually to the idea of location or placement and share a connection with the root ه–ن–ك, which expresses spatial distance. While هُنَاكَ serves as a general indicator of distance, هُنَالِكَ intensifies that reference, often used in elevated, dramatic, or eschatological contexts.
# هَـٰهُنَا
is a compound adverbial expression in Arabic meaning "here" or "right here", used to indicate a specific and immediate location. It combines the demonstrative particle هَا, used for emphasis or drawing attention, with هُنَا, which already means "here." The combination هَاهُنَا intensifies the sense of presence or immediacy, often translated as "right here" or "just here." This form appears in both classical and modern Arabic, including the Qur’an, such as in هَٰٓأَنتُمۡ هَٰٓؤُلَآءِ حَاجَجۡتُمۡ عَنۢهُمۡ فِي ٱلۡحَيَوٰةِ ٱلدُّنۡيَا فَمَن يُحَاجُّ ٱللَّهَ عَنۢهُمۡ يَوۡمَ ٱلۡقِيَٰمَةِۚ أَم مَّن يَكُونُ عَلَيۡهِمۡ وَكِيلٗا – and similar verses where immediacy and physical context are emphasized. Though the components come from recognizable parts (هَا + هُنَا), the full phrase هَاهُنَا is considered idiomatic and is treated as a fixed expression in grammar.
////others
# حيث
an adverb of place in Arabic, meaning "where," "wherever," or "in the place where." It is indeclinable (مَبْنِيّ) and not derived from a triliteral root. Unlike other interrogative or relative pronouns, حَيْثُ is not used to ask a question but rather to indicate location in statements or clauses. It often functions in relative clauses and may appear with added particles for nuance, such as مِنْ حَيْثُ ("from where") or إِلَىٰ حَيْثُ ("to where"). In classical and Qur’anic Arabic, حَيْثُ can carry a more abstract meaning of "in whatever situation" or "wherever it may be." For example: ٱللَّهُ يَأْتِي بِأَمْرِهِ حَيْثُ يَشَاءُ — "Allah brings His command wherever He wills." It does not change for case (nominative, accusative, genitive) and always appears in its base form.
//DUALS
# كلو
The words كِلْتَا and كِلَا are dual demonstrative-like nouns used to indicate "both" of something often appearing in iḍāfa (construct) form. كِلَا is used for masculine dual, and كِلْتَا is used for feminine dual. When attached to pronouns, they form expressions like كِلَاهُمَا ("both of them" — masculine or mixed gender).
//DEFECTIVE verbs
# كود
nearness, almost happening, or being on the verge of something.  an action was close to occurring but did not fully materialize.  كَادَ = "he almost" or "he was about to" do something. يَكَادُ = "he is almost" or "he is nearly". كَادُوا (kādū) = "they were near" or "they almost did" something. أَكَادُ = "I almost", emphasizing an individual's experience of nearly doing something. كَادَتْ = she was near. تَكَادُ = It almost.
